arXiv:2210.14095v2 [math.NT] 24 Aug 2023




On the distribution of partial quotients of reduced fractions with fixed denominator



Christoph Aistleitner

, 
Bence Borda

 and 
Manuel Hauke

Address: Graz University of Technology, Institute of Analysis and Number Theory, Steyrergasse 30/II, 8010 Graz, Austria

Email address: aistleitner@math.tugraz.at

Email address: borda@math.tugraz.at

Email address: hauke@math.tugraz.at



Abstract.
    

In this paper, we study distributional properties of the sequence of partial quotients in the continued fraction expansion of fractions  a/N a/Na/N, where  N NN is fixed and  a aa runs through the set of mod  N NN residue classes which are coprime with  N NN. Our methods cover statistics such as the sum of partial quotients, the maximal partial quotient, the empirical distribution of partial quotients, Dedekind sums, and much more. We prove a sharp concentration inequality for the sum of partial quotients, and sharp tail estimates for the maximal partial quotient and for Dedekind sums, all matching the tail behavior in the limit laws which are known under an extra averaging over the set of possible denominators  N NN. We show that the distribution of partial quotients of reduced fractions with fixed denominator gives a very good fit to the Gauß–Kuzmin distribution. As corollaries we establish the existence of reduced fractions with a small sum of partial quotients resp. a small maximal partial quotient.
  



Key words and phrases: Continued fractions, Diophantine approximation, Dedekind sum, Gauß–Kuzmin distribution


2020 Mathematics Subject ClassificationPrimary 11K50; Secondary 11N25, 68Q25




1. Introduction and statement of results




The theory of continued fractions plays a central role in Diophantine approximation, and is of major importance in many other mathematical areas such as algorithmics, dynamical systems, and geometry of numbers, to name only a few. Given an integer  N\geq 1 N≥1N\geq 1, throughout this paper  \mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} ℤN∗\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} denotes the set of all integers  1\leq a\leq N 1≤a≤N1\leq a\leq N which are coprime with  N NN. For any  N\geq 2 N≥2N\geq 2 and  a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} a∈ℤN∗a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}, we write  a/N=[0;a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{r}] a/N=[0;a1,a2,…,ar]a/N=[0;a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{r}] for the continued fraction expansion of  a/N a/Na/N, with the convention that we always use the canonical representation for which  a_{r}>1 ar>1a_{r}>1. We are interested in statistics such as the sum of partial quotients, the maximal partial quotient, or the number of partial quotients within a certain test window, when the denominator  N NN remains fixed and  a aa runs through the set  \mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} ℤN∗\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}.




Let





 S(a/N)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}a_{i} S⁡(a/N)=∑i=1raiS(a/N)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}a_{i}



denote the sum of partial quotients. This sum plays a crucial role in estimates for the algorithmic complexity of many important algorithms of an arithmetic nature, as well as for the discrepancy of Kronecker-type sets  (\frac{na\textup{ mod }N}{N})_{1\leq n\leq N} (n​a​ mod ​NN)1≤n≤N(\frac{na\textup{ mod }N}{N})_{1\leq n\leq N} (in dimension 1) and lattice point sets  (\frac{n}{N},\frac{na\textup{ mod }N}{N})_{1\leq n\leq N} (nN,n​a​ mod ​NN)1≤n≤N(\frac{n}{N},\frac{na\textup{ mod }N}{N})_{1\leq n\leq N} (in dimension 2). A number  a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} a∈ℤN∗a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} for which  S(a/N) S⁡(a/N)S(a/N) is small generates a set of “good lattice points”. The theory of quadrature rules for numerical integration based on such good lattice point sets was initiated by Hlawka [16] and Korobov [19] in the 1960s, and further developed by many other authors since then. For more details on this topic we refer the reader to [11, 27].




These examples motivate questions such as: Given a denominator  N NN, what is the smallest possible order of  S(a/N) S⁡(a/N)S(a/N) among all reduced fractions  a/N a/Na/N? What is the typical order of  S(a/N) S⁡(a/N)S(a/N), and how likely is the sum of partial quotients to deviate significantly from this typical behavior? How many partial quotients of a given size does a reduced fraction  a/N a/Na/N typically have? What is the smallest possible order of the maximal partial quotient of  a/N a/Na/N? Many questions of this type are addressed by the theorems in this paper.




The average value of  S(a/N) S⁡(a/N)S(a/N) was found independently by Panov [28] and Liehl [23], who showed that




(1)

 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}S\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\sim\frac{6}{\pi^{2}}(\log N)^{2}\qquad\textrm{as }N\to\infty, 1φ⁡(N)​∑a∈ℤN∗S⁡(aN)∼6π2​(log⁡N)2as ​N→∞,\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}S\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\sim\frac{6}{\pi^{2}}(\log N)^{2}\qquad\textrm{as }N\to\infty,



where  \varphi φ\varphi is the Euler totient function. A similar result for the average over all  1\leq a\leq N 1≤a≤N1\leq a\leq N (without the coprimality condition) was obtained by Yao and Knuth [35], who interpreted this as the average number of steps required by the subtractive greatest common divisor algorithm (see also the discussion in Section 4.5 of Knuth’s book [18]).




Our first theorem is a concentration inequality for the sum of partial quotients. The main message is that the typical order of  S(a/N) S⁡(a/N)S(a/N) is  (12/\pi^{2})\log N\log\log N (12/π2)​log⁡N​log⁡log⁡N(12/\pi^{2})\log N\log\log N, and that there is only a small proportion of fractions for which  S(a/N) S⁡(a/N)S(a/N) deviates significantly from this typical order. The fact that the average order  (\log N)^{2} (log⁡N)2(\log N)^{2} in equation (1) is much larger than the typical order  \log N\log\log N log⁡N​log⁡log​N\log N\log\log N in our theorem is caused by this small proportion of exceptional fractions, which might possess one or more unusually large partial quotients. In probabilistic terminology, this deviation between the average order and the typical order is caused by the fact that the distribution of partial quotients is heavy-tailed.



Theorem 1.



Let  C>0 C>0C>0 be arbitrary. For all  N\geq 3 N≥3N\geq 3 and  0<t\leq(\log N)^{C} 0<t≤(log⁡N)C0<t\leq(\log N)^{C}, we have





 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\left|\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,\left|S\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\log N\log\log N\right|\geq t\log N\right\}\right|\ll\frac{1}{t} 1φ⁡(N)​|{a∈ℤN∗:|S⁡(aN)−12π2​log​N​log⁡log⁡N|≥t​log​N}|≪1t\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\left|\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,\left|S\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\log N\log\log N\right|\geq t\log N\right\}\right|\ll\frac{1}{t}



with an implied constant depending only on  C CC.





This improves a result of Rukavishnikova [31, 32], who proved the upper tail estimate




(2)

 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\left|\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,S\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\geq t\log N\log\log N\right\}\right|\ll\frac{1}{t} 1φ⁡(N)​|{a∈ℤN∗:S⁡(aN)≥t​log⁡N​log⁡log⁡N}|≪1t\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\left|\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,S\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\geq t\log N\log\log N\right\}\right|\ll\frac{1}{t}



in the same range  0<t\leq(\log N)^{C} 0<t≤(log⁡N)C0<t\leq(\log N)^{C}. Rukavishnikova obtained her result by establishing an asymptotic upper bound for the average value of a restricted sum of partial quotients. In contrast, in this paper we establish a precise asymptotics for the average value, together with an upper bound for the variance of such restricted sums, which allows us to deduce the concentration inequality in Theorem 1.




Theorem 1 should be compared to recent results of Bettin and Drappeau [4], who proved that under an additional averaging over the set of all denominators  N NN up to a certain threshold, the distribution of a suitably centered and rescaled version of  S(a/N) S⁡(a/N)S(a/N) converges to a limit distribution. More precisely, when considering all reduced fractions  a/N a/Na/N in  [0,1] [0,1][0,1] for which  N\leq Q N≤QN\leq Q (the so-called Farey fractions of order  Q QQ, denoted by  F_{Q} FQF_{Q}), then for all real  t tt,





 \lim_{Q\to\infty}\frac{1}{|F_{Q}|}\left|\left\{\frac{a}{N}\in F_{Q}\,:\,\frac{S(a/N)-\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\log N\log\log N}{\log N}\leq t\right\}\right|=F(t), limQ→∞1|FQ|​|{aN∈FQ:S⁡(a/N)−12π2​log⁡N​log⁡log⁡Nlog⁡N≤t}|=F⁡(t),\lim_{Q\to\infty}\frac{1}{|F_{Q}|}\left|\left\{\frac{a}{N}\in F_{Q}\,:\,\frac{S(a/N)-\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\log N\log\log N}{\log N}\leq t\right\}\right|=F(t),



where  F(t) F⁡(t)F(t) is the cumulative distribution function of a specific stable law with stability parameter  1 11. In particular, the tail probabilities are of order  1-F(t)\sim c/t 1−F⁡(t)∼c/t1-F(t)\sim c/t with some constant  c>0 c>0c>0 as  t\to\infty t→∞t\to\infty, matching the tail estimate in our Theorem 1 for the case of fixed denominators. Therefore Theorem 1 is optimal up to the value of the implied constant.




We note that the methods of Bettin and Drappeau are very different from the ones used in the present paper, and rely on a dynamical systems approach which builds upon earlier work of Dolgopyat [12] and Baladi and Vallée [2]. It remains unclear if a suitably centered and rescaled version of  S(a/N) S⁡(a/N)S(a/N) has a limit distribution when  N NN is fixed, and if such a limiting distributional behavior would have to take into account certain arithmetic properties of  N NN (an arithmetic effect which is possibly lost when taking an additional average over all denominators  N NN).




As an immediate consequence of Theorem 1, we have the following corollary.



Corollary 2.



For all  N\geq 3 N≥3N\geq 3, there exists a reduced fraction  a/N a/Na/N for which





 S\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\leq\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\log N\log\log N+O(\log N). S⁡(aN)≤12π2​log⁡N​log⁡log⁡N+O⁡(log⁡N).S\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\leq\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\log N\log\log N+O(\log N).







To put the conclusion of the corollary into perspective, we note that the tail estimate (2) of Rukavishnikova only guarantees the existence of a reduced fraction  a/N a/Na/N for which  S(a/N)\ll\log N\log\log N S⁡(a/N)≪log⁡N​log⁡log⁡NS(a/N)\ll\log N\log\log N with an unspecified implied constant. We also note that a well-known lower bound states that  S(a/N)\gg\log N S⁡(a/N)≫log⁡NS(a/N)\gg\log N for all reduced fractions  a/N a/Na/N, so Corollary 2 is optimal up to the double logarithmic factor.




The next result is a tail estimate for the maximal partial quotient of  a/N a/Na/N, denoted by





 M\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)=\max_{1\leq i\leq r}a_{i}. M⁡(aN)=max1≤i≤r⁡ai.M\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)=\max_{1\leq i\leq r}a_{i}.





Theorem 3.



Let  C>0 C>0C>0 be arbitrary. For all  N\geq 3 N≥3N\geq 3 and  0<t\leq(\log N)^{C} 0<t≤(log⁡N)C0<t\leq(\log N)^{C}, we have





 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\left|\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,M\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\geq t\log N\right\}\right|\leq\frac{12}{\pi^{2}t}+O\left(\frac{(\log\log N)^{3}}{t\log N}\right) 1φ⁡(N)​|{a∈ℤN∗:M⁡(aN)≥t​log⁡N}|≤12π2​t+O⁡((log⁡log⁡N)3t​log⁡N)\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\left|\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,M\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\geq t\log N\right\}\right|\leq\frac{12}{\pi^{2}t}+O\left(\frac{(\log\log N)^{3}}{t\log N}\right)



with an implied constant depending only on  C CC.





This should be compared with a result of Hensley [14, Theorem 1], who found the limit distribution of a rescaled version of  M(a/N) M⁡(a/N)M(a/N) when an additional average over  N NN is taken. More precisely, again writing  F_{Q} FQF_{Q} for the set of Farey fractions of order  Q QQ, for all  t>0 t>0t>0 we have





 \lim_{Q\to\infty}\frac{1}{|F_{Q}|}\left|\left\{\frac{a}{N}\in F_{Q}\,:\,M\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\geq t\log N\right\}\right|=1-e^{-\frac{12}{\pi^{2}t}}. limQ→∞1|FQ|​|{aN∈FQ:M⁡(aN)≥t​log⁡N}|=1−e−12π2​t.\lim_{Q\to\infty}\frac{1}{|F_{Q}|}\left|\left\{\frac{a}{N}\in F_{Q}\,:\,M\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\geq t\log N\right\}\right|=1-e^{-\frac{12}{\pi^{2}t}}.



Note that





 1-e^{-\frac{12}{\pi^{2}t}}=\frac{12}{\pi^{2}t}+O\left(\frac{1}{t^{2}}\right)\qquad\text{as $t\to\infty$}, 1−e−12π2​t=12π2​t+O⁡(1t2)as t→∞,1-e^{-\frac{12}{\pi^{2}t}}=\frac{12}{\pi^{2}t}+O\left(\frac{1}{t^{2}}\right)\qquad\text{as $t\to\infty$},



so for large  t tt the tail estimate for fixed  N NN provided by our Theorem 3 perfectly matches the tail behavior after taking an additional average over all denominators, even including the correct constant factor  12/\pi^{2} 12/π212/\pi^{2}. As in the discussion following Theorem 1, it remains unclear whether the additional average over all denominators is necessary to obtain a limit distribution; that is, whether there exists an analogue of Hensley’s theorem which holds for fixed denominators, and whether such a result would be universal, or rather have to account for certain arithmetic properties of  N NN.




From Theorem 3 we immediately obtain the following corollary.



Corollary 4.



For all  N\geq 3 N≥3N\geq 3, there exists a reduced fraction  a/N a/Na/N for which





 M(a/N)\leq\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\log N+O((\log\log N)^{3}). M⁡(a/N)≤12π2​log⁡N+O⁡((log⁡log⁡N)3).M(a/N)\leq\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\log N+O((\log\log N)^{3}).







To the best of our knowledge, the fact that for all  N NN there exists a reduced fraction  a/N a/Na/N with  M(a/N)\ll\log N M⁡(a/N)≪log⁡NM(a/N)\ll\log N was first proved by Korobov [19] for prime  N NN and by Zaremba [36] for composite  N NN. In [9] it was shown that for all  N NN there exists a reduced fraction  a/N a/Na/N with  M(a/N)\leq 3\log N M⁡(a/N)≤3​log⁡NM(a/N)\leq 3\log N, which appears to have been the strongest known existence result so far (note for comparison that  12/\pi^{2}\approx 1.22 12/π2≈1.2212/\pi^{2}\approx 1.22). Zaremba’s famous conjecture asserts that actually for all  N NN there is a reduced fraction  a/N a/Na/N with  M(a/N)\leq K M⁡(a/N)≤KM(a/N)\leq K, where  K KK is a suitable absolute constant (it is conjectured that  K=5 K=5K=5 is admissible). Zaremba’s conjecture was established when  N NN is of very special type; most importantly, Niederreiter [26] proved the conjecture in the case when  N NN is a power of a small prime. The conjecture has been confirmed by Bourgain and Kontorovich [5] for a set of denominators having full asymptotic density; see also [13, 17]. In the recent preprint [24], the estimate  M(a/N)\ll\log N M⁡(a/N)≪log⁡NM(a/N)\ll\log N was improved to  M(a/N)\ll\log N/\log\log N M⁡(a/N)≪log⁡N/log⁡log⁡NM(a/N)\ll\log N/\log\log N for a wide range of denominators  N NN. However, as a statement concerning all possible denominators  N NN, Zaremba’s conjecture remains wide open.




Given integers  1\leq b\leq c 1≤b≤c1\leq b\leq c, let





 L_{[b,c]}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathds{1}_{\{b\leq a_{i}\leq c\}} L[b,c](aN)=∑i=1r𝟙{b≤ai≤c}L_{[b,c]}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathds{1}_{\{b\leq a_{i}\leq c\}}



denote the number of partial quotients of  a/N a/Na/N which fall in the interval  [b,c] [b,c][b,c]. Since all partial quotients of  a/N a/Na/N are at most  N NN, choosing e.g.  c=N c=Nc=N corresponds to counting partial quotients in the interval  [b,\infty) [b,∞)[b,\infty), that is,  L_{[b,N]}(a/N)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathds{1}_{\{a_{i}\geq b\}} L[b,N](a/N)=∑i=1r𝟙{ai≥b}L_{[b,N]}(a/N)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathds{1}_{\{a_{i}\geq b\}}. In our next result we find the precise asymptotics of the average value, and an upper bound for the variance of  L_{[b,c]}(a/N) L[b,c]​(a/N)L_{[b,c]}(a/N). In particular, for large values of  b bb,  L_{[b,c]}(a/N) L[b,c]​(a/N)L_{[b,c]}(a/N) concentrates around its mean for the majority of reduced fractions  a/N a/Na/N.



Theorem 5.



Let  C>0 C>0C>0 be arbitrary. For all  N\geq 3 N≥3N\geq 3 and  1\leq b\leq c 1≤b≤c1\leq b\leq c with  b\leq(\log N)^{C} b≤(log⁡N)Cb\leq(\log N)^{C}, we have




(3)

 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}L_{[b,c]}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)=\mu_{[b,c]}\log N+O\left(\frac{(\log\log N)^{3}}{b}\right), 1φ⁡(N)​∑a∈ℤN∗L[b,c]​(aN)=μ[b,c]​log⁡N+O⁡((log⁡log⁡N)3b),\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}L_{[b,c]}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)=\mu_{[b,c]}\log N+O\left(\frac{(\log\log N)^{3}}{b}\right),



and




(4)

 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\left(L_{[b,c]}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-\mu_{[b,c]}\log N\right)^{2}\ll\frac{(\log N)^{2}}{b^{4}}+\frac{\log N\log\log N}{b} 1φ⁡(N)​∑a∈ℤN∗(L[b,c]​(aN)−μ[b,c]​log⁡N)2≪(log⁡N)2b4+log⁡N​log⁡log​Nb\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\left(L_{[b,c]}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-\mu_{[b,c]}\log N\right)^{2}\ll\frac{(\log N)^{2}}{b^{4}}+\frac{\log N\log\log N}{b}



with  \mu_{[b,c]}=\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\sum_{m=b}^{c}\log\left(1+\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\right) μ[b,c]=12π2​∑m=bclog⁡(1+1m⁡(m+2))\mu_{[b,c]}=\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\sum_{m=b}^{c}\log\left(1+\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\right) and implied constants depending only on  C CC.





By a result of Baladi and Vallée [2, Theorem 3(a)] (see also Bettin and Drappeau [4, Corollary 3.2]), under an extra averaging over the denominators  N NN we once again have a limit distribution: for any fixed  1\leq b\leq c 1≤b≤c1\leq b\leq c,





 \frac{L_{[b,c]}(a/N)-\mu_{[b,c]}\log N}{\sigma_{[b,c]}\sqrt{\log N}} L[b,c]​(a/N)−μ[b,c]​log⁡Nσ[b,c]​log⁡N\frac{L_{[b,c]}(a/N)-\mu_{[b,c]}\log N}{\sigma_{[b,c]}\sqrt{\log N}}



with a suitable  \sigma_{[b,c]}>0 σ[b,c]>0\sigma_{[b,c]}>0 converges to the standard normal distribution. It remains open whether a similar central limit theorem holds for reduced fractions with a fixed denominator. Avdeeva and Bykovskiĭ [1] applied the results of Baladi and Vallée to establish (as a special case) concentration of  L_{[b,c]} L[b,c]L_{[b,c]} around its mean, but it is not clear if their method allows the computation of first and second moments as in our theorem.




Note that Theorem 5 is precise enough to yield a sensible result even in the case when  b=c=m b=c=mb=c=m, i.e. when we are counting the number of occurrences of a particular value  m mm for the partial quotients. In this case, the theorem gives





 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathds{1}_{\{a_{i}=m\}}=\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\right)\log N+O\left(\frac{(\log\log N)^{3}}{m}\right), 1φ⁡(N)∑a∈ℤN∗∑i=1r𝟙{ai=m}=12π2log(1+1m⁡(m+2))logN+O((log⁡log⁡N)3m),\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathds{1}_{\{a_{i}=m\}}=\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\log\left(1+\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\right)\log N+O\left(\frac{(\log\log N)^{3}}{m}\right),



and the main term (which is of order  m^{-2}\log N m−2​log⁡Nm^{-2}\log N) dominates the error as long as  m mm is moderate in comparison with  \log N log⁡N\log N. It is natural to divide the equation by a factor of  \frac{12\log 2}{\pi^{2}}\log N 12​log⁡2π2​log⁡N\frac{12\log 2}{\pi^{2}}\log N, which is the asymptotic order of the average length of the continued fraction of a reduced fraction with denominator  N NN (see e.g. [29]), which leads to





 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\frac{\pi^{2}}{12\log 2\log N}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathds{1}_{\{a_{i}=m\}}=\frac{\log\left(1+\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\right)}{\log 2}+O\left(\frac{(\log\log N)^{3}}{m\log N}\right). 1φ⁡(N)π212​log⁡2​log​N∑a∈ℤN∗∑i=1r𝟙{ai=m}=log⁡(1+1m⁡(m+2))log⁡2+O((log⁡log⁡N)3m​log⁡N).\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\frac{\pi^{2}}{12\log 2\log N}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathds{1}_{\{a_{i}=m\}}=\frac{\log\left(1+\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\right)}{\log 2}+O\left(\frac{(\log\log N)^{3}}{m\log N}\right).



The main term on the right-hand side of this equation is the probability of the number  m mm in the Gauß–Kuzmin distribution, which is the probability distribution governing the distribution of the partial quotients of random reals. Thus, informally speaking, Theorem 5 asserts that the distribution of the partial quotients of reduced fractions with fixed denominator is in very good accordance with the distribution coming from the corresponding random model. Versions of the “expected value” part of Theorem 5, i.e. equation (3), have been established by other authors before. In particular, Ustinov [33] obtained a version of (3) with an error term which is much smaller than ours in terms of  N NN, but depends on  b bb and  c cc in an unspecified way. David and Shapira [10] proved a qualitative version of (3), without giving an error term, using methods from ergodic theory.




Our final result is a tail estimate for Dedekind sums, which are defined for  a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} a∈ℤN∗a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} as





 D\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)=\sum_{b=1}^{N-1}\left(\frac{b}{N}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\left(\left\{\frac{ba}{N}\right\}-\frac{1}{2}\right), D⁡(aN)=∑b=1N−1(bN−12)​({b​aN}−12),D\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)=\sum_{b=1}^{N-1}\left(\frac{b}{N}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\left(\left\{\frac{ba}{N}\right\}-\frac{1}{2}\right),



where  \{\cdot\} {⋅}\{\cdot\} denotes the fractional part function. Dedekind sums are a classical object in analytic number theory, and appear most importantly in the modularity relation of the Dedekind eta function; see for example [30] for more background. An identity due to Barkan [3] and independently Hickerson [15] relates  D(a/N) D⁡(a/N)D(a/N) to the partial quotients via




(5)

 \begin{split}D\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)&=\frac{(-1)^{r}-1}{8}+\frac{1}{12}\left(\frac{a}{N}-(-1)^{r}[0;a_{r},\ldots,a_{2},a_{1}]-S_{\mathrm{alt}}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\right)\\
&=-\frac{1}{12}S_{\mathrm{alt}}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)+O(1),\end{split} D⁡(aN)=(−1)r−18+112​(aN−(−1)r​[0;ar,…,a2,a1]−Salt​(aN))=−112​Salt​(aN)+O⁡(1),\begin{split}D\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)&=\frac{(-1)^{r}-1}{8}+\frac{1}{12}\left(\frac{a}{N}-(-1)^{r}[0;a_{r},\ldots,a_{2},a_{1}]-S_{\mathrm{alt}}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\right)\\
&=-\frac{1}{12}S_{\mathrm{alt}}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)+O(1),\end{split}



where  S_{\mathrm{alt}}(a/N)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}(-1)^{i}a_{i} Salt​(a/N)=∑i=1r(−1)i​aiS_{\mathrm{alt}}(a/N)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}(-1)^{i}a_{i} is the alternating sum of partial quotients.



Theorem 6.



Let  C>0 C>0C>0 be arbitrary. For all  N\geq 3 N≥3N\geq 3 and  0<t\leq(\log N)^{C} 0<t≤(log⁡N)C0<t\leq(\log N)^{C}, we have





 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\left|\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,\left|D\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\right|\geq t\log N\right\}\right|\ll\frac{1}{t} 1φ⁡(N)​|{a∈ℤN∗:|D⁡(aN)|≥t​log⁡N}|≪1t\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\left|\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,\left|D\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\right|\geq t\log N\right\}\right|\ll\frac{1}{t}



with an implied constant depending only on  C CC.





Vardi [34] showed that under an additional averaging over the denominators, the normalized Dedekind sum  2\pi D(a/N)/\log N 2​π​D​(a/N)/log⁡N2\pi D(a/N)/\log N converges to the Cauchy distribution. Note that our tail estimate  1/t 1/t1/t in Theorem 6 perfectly matches the order of the tail probabilities of the Cauchy distribution. Once again, it is not clear whether the extra averaging over denominators is necessary for the existence of a limit law.




We add some further references to results which are related to those in the present paper. Rukavishnikova [31] proved a second moment bound for the average sum of partial quotients when  N NN is fixed and  a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N} a∈ℤNa\in\mathbb{Z}_{N} (i.e., without a coprimality assumption as in our theorems). In the same setup, Bykovskiĭ [6] proved a second moment bound for the length (which corresponds to choosing  b=1 b=1b=1 and  c=N c=Nc=N in our Theorem 5) of the continued fraction expansion. The first moment of the length of the continued fraction, in the coprime setup, was calculated with high precision in [7]. Several authors studied problems related to those in the present paper when the average is taken over a (sufficiently large) multiplicative subgroup of  \mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} ℤN∗\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}, see for example [8, 25].




We now give an outline of the remaining parts of this paper. The key tools in this paper come from sieve theory and uniform distribution (resp. discrepancy) theory, of course in combination with results from Diophantine approximation and the theory of continued fractions. In Section 2, we estimate the discrepancy of the set of reduced fractions  \{a/N\,:\,a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\} {a/N:a∈ℤN∗}\{a/N\,:\,a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\} using sieve theory. In Section 3, we introduce the main auxiliary object of the paper, the restricted sum  \sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathds{1}_{\{\eta\leq a_{i}\leq\theta\}}f(a_{i}) ∑i=1r𝟙{η≤ai≤θ}f(ai)\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathds{1}_{\{\eta\leq a_{i}\leq\theta\}}f(a_{i}) with cutoff parameters  \eta η\eta and  \theta θ\theta and a non-negative, non-decreasing function  f ff. We show how the first and second moment of the restricted sum can be approximated by sums of certain weight functions. In Section 4, we find the average of such a restricted sum up to a small error, and in Section 5 we give an upper bound for the variance of the restricted sum. In Section 6, we deduce Theorems 1, 3 and 5 from our general results on restricted sums, by specializing to  f(x)=x f⁡(x)=xf(x)=x and to  f(x)=1 f⁡(x)=1f(x)=1, respectively. As the overview of the proof strategy indicates, our methods in fact apply to very general sums of the form  \sum_{i=1}^{r}f(a_{i}) ∑i=1rf⁡(ai)\sum_{i=1}^{r}f(a_{i}), yielding sharp tail estimates or concentration inequalities depending on the growth rate of  f ff. Finally, in Section 7 we explain how to modify our approach for alternating sums  \sum_{i=1}^{r}(-1)^{i}f(a_{i}) ∑i=1r(−1)i​f​(ai)\sum_{i=1}^{r}(-1)^{i}f(a_{i}), and prove Theorem 6 on Dedekind sums.




Before turning to the proofs, we note that our arguments crucially exploit the existence of a certain bijective mapping  \mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\to\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} ℤN∗→ℤN∗\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\to\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} which is very similar to the map sending  a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} a∈ℤN∗a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} to its modular inverse, and the fact that the normalized counting measure on  \mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} ℤN∗\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} is invariant under this mapping (cf. the discussion before the statement of Lemma 10 below). For this reason, our method does not allow us to replace the uniform average  \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}} 1φ⁡(N)∑a∈ℤN∗\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}} by some weighted average, say for example with respect to the normalized counting measure on  \{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*},~a\leq\eta N\} {a∈ℤN∗,a≤η​N}\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*},~a\leq\eta N\} with some fixed  \eta\in(0,1) η∈(0,1)\eta\in(0,1). It seems that proving an analogue of our theorems for such weighted averages (following the methods used in this paper) would need significant additional input on the distribution of modular inverses in short intervals, or estimates for Kloosterman sums in short intervals.





2. A discrepancy estimate for the set of reduced fractions




Since we work with reduced fractions, sieve methods naturally play an important role. For the convenience of the reader we recall the fundamental lemma of sieve theory in the formulation of [20, Theorem 18.11].



Lemma 7 (Fundamental lemma of sieve theory).



Let  (a_{n})_{n\geq 1} (an)n≥1(a_{n})_{n\geq 1} be non-negative reals, such that  \sum_{n=1}^{\infty}a_{n}<\infty ∑n=1∞an<∞\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}a_{n}<\infty. Assume that there exist a multiplicative function  g gg such that  0\leq g(p)<p 0≤g⁡(p)<p0\leq g(p)<p for all  p\in\mathcal{P} p∈𝒫p\in\mathcal{P}, a real number  x xx, and positive reals  c_{1},c_{2} c1,c2c_{1},c_{2} such that





 A_{d}=:x\frac{g(d)}{d}+r_{d},\qquad d\mid P, Ad=:xg⁡(d)d+rd,d∣P,A_{d}=:x\frac{g(d)}{d}+r_{d},\qquad d\mid P,



and





 \prod_{p\in(y_{1},y_{2}]\cap\mathcal{P}}\left(1-\frac{g(p)}{p}\right)^{-1}<\left(\frac{\log y_{2}}{\log y_{1}}\right)^{c_{1}}\left(1+\frac{c_{2}}{\log y_{1}}\right),\qquad 3/2\leq y_{1}\leq y_{2}\leq y. ∏p∈(y1,y2]∩𝒫(1−g⁡(p)p)−1<(log⁡y2log⁡y1)c1​(1+c2log⁡y1),3/2≤y1≤y2≤y.\prod_{p\in(y_{1},y_{2}]\cap\mathcal{P}}\left(1-\frac{g(p)}{p}\right)^{-1}<\left(\frac{\log y_{2}}{\log y_{1}}\right)^{c_{1}}\left(1+\frac{c_{2}}{\log y_{1}}\right),\qquad 3/2\leq y_{1}\leq y_{2}\leq y.



For any real  u\geq 1 u≥1u\geq 1,





 \sum_{\mathrm{gcd}(n,P)=1}a_{n}=\left(1+O(u^{-u/2})\right)x\prod_{p\in\mathcal{P}}\left(1-\frac{g(p)}{p}\right)+O\left(\sum_{d\leq y^{u},~d\mid P}|r_{d}|\right) ∑gcd⁡(n,P)=1an=(1+O(u−u/2))x∏p∈𝒫(1−g⁡(p)p)+O(∑d≤yu,d|P|rd|)\sum_{\mathrm{gcd}(n,P)=1}a_{n}=\left(1+O(u^{-u/2})\right)x\prod_{p\in\mathcal{P}}\left(1-\frac{g(p)}{p}\right)+O\left(\sum_{d\leq y^{u},~d\mid P}|r_{d}|\right)

We will only use the fundamental lemma with  g=1 g=1g=1. In this case the constants  c_{1},c_{2} c1,c2c_{1},c_{2}, and consequently the implied constants in the claim are uniform in the sequence  (a_{n})_{n\geq 1} (an)n≥1(a_{n})_{n\geq 1} and the set of primes  \mathcal{P} 𝒫\mathcal{P}.




We now prove a discrepancy estimate for the set of reduced fractions with a fixed denominator  N NN. Note that the corresponding result for the set of all (not necessarily reduced) fractions with a fixed denominator  N NN is trivial. 3. Weight functions




Throughout the following sections, we fix integers  N\geq 3 N≥3N\geq 3 and  1\leq\eta\leq\theta 1≤η≤θ1\leq\eta\leq\theta, and a non-decreasing function  f:\mathbb{N}\to[0,\infty) f:ℕ→[0,∞)f:\mathbb{N}\to[0,\infty). As noted in the Introduction, the continued fraction expansion of  a/N a/Na/N,  a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} a∈ℤN∗a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} is written as  a/N=[0;a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{r}] a/N=[0;a1,a2,…,ar]a/N=[0;a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{r}], where the partial quotients  a_{i}=a_{i}(a/N) ai=ai​(a/N)a_{i}=a_{i}(a/N) and the length  r=r(a/N) r=r⁡(a/N)r=r(a/N) are functions of  a/N a/Na/N. The convergents to  a/N a/Na/N are denoted by  p_{i}/q_{i}=[0;a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{i}] pi/qi=[0;a1,a2,…,ai]p_{i}/q_{i}=[0;a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{i}],  0\leq i\leq r 0≤i≤r0\leq i\leq r (here,  a_{i}=1 ai=1a_{i}=1 is possible), where the convergent numerators  p_{i}=p_{i}(a/N) pi=pi​(a/N)p_{i}=p_{i}(a/N) and denominators  q_{i}=q_{i}(a/N) qi=qi​(a/N)q_{i}=q_{i}(a/N) are likewise functions of  a/N a/Na/N. The main object in the following sections is the restricted sum




(7)

 S_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right):=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathds{1}_{\{\eta\leq a_{i}\leq\theta\}}f(a_{i}). Sf,η,θ(aN):=∑i=1r𝟙{η≤ai≤θ}f(ai).S_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right):=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathds{1}_{\{\eta\leq a_{i}\leq\theta\}}f(a_{i}).






Given any integers  k\geq 1 k≥1k\geq 1,  b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*} b∈ℤk∗b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*} and  m\geq 1 m≥1m\geq 1, we define intervals  I(b/k,m) I⁡(b/k,m)I(b/k,m) and  I^{\prime}(b/k,m) I′​(b/k,m)I^{\prime}(b/k,m) as follows. We introduce the weight function  w_{f,\eta,\theta}(b/k,x) wf,η,θ​(b/k,x)w_{f,\eta,\theta}(b/k,x), which is defined as

Before we continue with the formal proofs, we give an outline of the general strategy. To study the sum of partial quotients  S(a/N) S⁡(a/N)S(a/N), we will use  f(x)=x f⁡(x)=xf(x)=x. Finding the expected value and estimating the variance of the restricted sums  S_{f,\eta,\theta} Sf,η,θS_{f,\eta,\theta} as defined in (7) for this choice of  f ff and for a wide range of parameters  \eta,\theta η,θ\eta,\theta will allow us to understand the distribution of the unrestricted sum  S(a/N) S⁡(a/N)S(a/N) as well. Note that the cutoff at  \theta θ\theta is necessary to obtain meaningful results, since (as mentioned in the Introduction) otherwise the average order of the sum of partial quotients would be dominated by the contribution of very few fractions  a/N a/Na/N that possess unusually large partial quotients, thereby disrupting the concentration effect which we are trying to detect.




We will take a sum of  w_{f,\eta,\theta}(b/k,a/N) wf,η,θ​(b/k,a/N)w_{f,\eta,\theta}(b/k,a/N) over all  a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} a∈ℤN∗a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}, all  b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*} b∈ℤk∗b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}, and finally over all  k<N k<Nk<N. To actually calculate this sum, we use the regularity of the distribution of the sets of reduced fractions  (a/N)_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}} (a/N)a∈ℤN∗(a/N)_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}} resp.  (b/k)_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}} (b/k)b∈ℤk∗(b/k)_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}} as asserted by Lemma 8, and the fact that by Lemma 9 a sum of function evaluations can be efficiently approximated by an integral, assuming the regularity of the set of sampling points. To estimate the number of partial quotients  L_{[b,c]}(a/N) L[b,c]​(a/N)L_{[b,c]}(a/N) which fall into the interval  [b,c] [b,c][b,c], we use a similar reasoning but with  f(x)=1 f⁡(x)=1f(x)=1,  \eta=b η=b\eta=b and  \theta=c θ=c\theta=c instead of  f(x)=x f⁡(x)=xf(x)=x. We note that some of the basic ideas of this approach are taken from papers of Larcher [22] and Rukavishnikova [31, 32].




The following two lemmas describe how to estimate the first and second moments of the restricted sum  S_{f,\eta,\theta}(a/N) Sf,η,θ​(a/N)S_{f,\eta,\theta}(a/N) in terms of the weight function  w_{f,\eta,\theta} wf,η,θw_{f,\eta,\theta}. A key point of the lemmas is the fact that the summation over  k kk only runs up to  \sqrt{N} N\sqrt{N}, and not all the way up to  N NN; this is crucial since for  k kk of size near  \sqrt{N} N\sqrt{N} the errors coming from the discrepancy bounds in Lemmas 8 and 9 start being too large. The intuitive explanation why such a reduction for the range of  k kk is possible is that the continued fraction expansion  [0;a_{1},\dots,a_{r}] [0;a1,…,ar][0;a_{1},\dots,a_{r}] can bijectively be flipped around to give the continued fraction  [0;a_{r},\dots,a_{1}] [0;ar,…,a1][0;a_{r},\dots,a_{1}] of a reduced fraction with the same denominator. Consequently, when summing over all possible values  a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*} a∈ℤN∗a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}, every admissible continued fraction expansion occurs once in “correct” order and once in reflected order as well, so that instead of taking a statistics of all partial quotients of  a/N a/Na/N, we can instead only consider those partial quotients associated to convergent denominators going up to  \sqrt{N} N\sqrt{N}, and then compensate for this reduced range by noting that the remaining partial quotients appear at the initial segment of the reflected continued fraction (so that we get the correct result when restricting to  k<\sqrt{N} k<Nk<\sqrt{N} and compensating for this by multiplying the overall count by a factor of 2). However, the actual situation is slightly more complicated because  [0;a_{r},\dots,a_{1}] [0;ar,…,a1][0;a_{r},\dots,a_{1}] is not an admissible continued fraction expansion if  a_{1}=1 a1=1a_{1}=1, and because we have to be careful that partial quotients which are associated with convergent denominators close to  \sqrt{N} N\sqrt{N} are neither omitted nor counted twice when invoking the reflection argument.



Lemma 10.



Assume that  \sqrt{N}>\theta+2 N>θ+2\sqrt{N}>\theta+2. Then





 \begin{split}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}S_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)&=2\sum_{1\leq k<\sqrt{N}}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}}w_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},\frac{a}{N}\right)\\
&\quad+O\left(N\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}\frac{f(m)}{m^{2}}+\sum_{\frac{\sqrt{N}}{\theta+2}<k<\sqrt{N}}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}}w_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},\frac{a}{N}\right)\right)\end{split} ∑a∈ℤN∗Sf,η,θ​(aN)=2​∑1≤k<N∑a∈ℤN∗∑b∈ℤk∗wf,η,θ​(bk,aN)+O⁡(N​∑m=ηθf⁡(m)m2+∑Nθ+2<k<N∑a∈ℤN∗∑b∈ℤk∗wf,η,θ​(bk,aN))\begin{split}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}S_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)&=2\sum_{1\leq k<\sqrt{N}}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}}w_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},\frac{a}{N}\right)\\
&\quad+O\left(N\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}\frac{f(m)}{m^{2}}+\sum_{\frac{\sqrt{N}}{\theta+2}<k<\sqrt{N}}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}}w_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},\frac{a}{N}\right)\right)\end{split}



with an absolute implied constant.

4. Expected value




In this section we use Lemma 10 to determine the expected value of  S_{f,\eta,\theta} Sf,η,θS_{f,\eta,\theta}.



Theorem 12.



Let  N\geq 3 N≥3N\geq 3 and  1\leq\eta\leq\theta 1≤η≤θ1\leq\eta\leq\theta be integers, and let  f:\mathbb{N}\to[0,\infty) f:ℕ→[0,∞)f:\mathbb{N}\to[0,\infty) be a non-decreasing function with  f(\theta)>0 f⁡(θ)>0f(\theta)>0. Let





 A_{f,\eta,\theta}=\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}f(m)\log\left(1+\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\right),\quad B_{f,\eta,\theta}=A_{f,\eta,\theta}+\frac{f(\theta)}{\theta},\quad C_{f,\eta,\theta}=\frac{5f(\theta)}{B_{f,\eta,\theta}}. Af,η,θ=12π2​∑m=ηθf⁡(m)​log⁡(1+1m⁡(m+2)),Bf,η,θ=Af,η,θ+f⁡(θ)θ,Cf,η,θ=5​f​(θ)Bf,η,θ.A_{f,\eta,\theta}=\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}f(m)\log\left(1+\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\right),\quad B_{f,\eta,\theta}=A_{f,\eta,\theta}+\frac{f(\theta)}{\theta},\quad C_{f,\eta,\theta}=\frac{5f(\theta)}{B_{f,\eta,\theta}}.

Lemma 13.



For any integer  k\geq 1 k≥1k\geq 1,





 \sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}}\int_{0}^{1}w_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},x\right)\,\mathrm{d}x=\frac{2\varphi(k)}{k^{2}}\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}f(m)\log\left(1+\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\right)+O\left(\frac{\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}f(m)/m^{2}}{k(\log(k+1))^{100}}\right) ∑b∈ℤk∗∫01wf,η,θ​(bk,x)​𝑑x=2​φ​(k)k2​∑m=ηθf⁡(m)​log⁡(1+1m⁡(m+2))+O⁡(∑m=ηθf⁡(m)/m2k​(log⁡(k+1))100)\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}}\int_{0}^{1}w_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},x\right)\,\mathrm{d}x=\frac{2\varphi(k)}{k^{2}}\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}f(m)\log\left(1+\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\right)+O\left(\frac{\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}f(m)/m^{2}}{k(\log(k+1))^{100}}\right)



with an absolute implied constant.




Proof.



The proof for  k=1 k=1k=1 is trivial, thus we may assume that  k\geq 2 k≥2k\geq 2. Fix  b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*} b∈ℤk∗b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}, and consider the continued fraction expansion  b/k=[0;b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{s}] b/k=[0;b1,b2,…,bs]b/k=[0;b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{s}] with  b_{s}>1 bs>1b_{s}>1. Let  p_{i}/q_{i}=[0;b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{i}] pi/qi=[0;b1,b2,…,bi]p_{i}/q_{i}=[0;b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{i}] denote the convergents. Recall the identity  p_{s}q_{s-1}-p_{s-1}q_{s}=(-1)^{s+1} ps​qs−1−ps−1​qs=(−1)s+1p_{s}q_{s-1}-p_{s-1}q_{s}=(-1)^{s+1}. We have





 \int_{0}^{1}w_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},x\right)\,\mathrm{d}x=\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}f(m)\left(\lambda(I(b/k,m))+\lambda(I^{\prime}(b/k,m))\right), ∫01wf,η,θ​(bk,x)​𝑑x=∑m=ηθf⁡(m)​(λ⁡(I⁡(b/k,m))+λ⁡(I′​(b/k,m))),\int_{0}^{1}w_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},x\right)\,\mathrm{d}x=\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}f(m)\left(\lambda(I(b/k,m))+\lambda(I^{\prime}(b/k,m))\right),



where





 \begin{split}\lambda(I(b/k,m))&=\left|[0;b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{s},m]-[0;b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{s},m+1]\right|\\
&=\left|\frac{mp_{s}+p_{s-1}}{mq_{s}+q_{s-1}}-\frac{(m+1)p_{s}+p_{s-1}}{(m+1)q_{s}+q_{s-1}}\right|\\
&=\frac{1}{(mq_{s}+q_{s-1})((m+1)q_{s}+q_{s-1})},\end{split} λ⁡(I⁡(b/k,m))=|[0;b1,b2,…,bs,m]−[0;b1,b2,…,bs,m+1]|=|m​ps+ps−1m​qs+qs−1−(m+1)​ps+ps−1(m+1)​qs+qs−1|=1(m​qs+qs−1)​((m+1)​qs+qs−1),\begin{split}\lambda(I(b/k,m))&=\left|[0;b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{s},m]-[0;b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{s},m+1]\right|\\
&=\left|\frac{mp_{s}+p_{s-1}}{mq_{s}+q_{s-1}}-\frac{(m+1)p_{s}+p_{s-1}}{(m+1)q_{s}+q_{s-1}}\right|\\
&=\frac{1}{(mq_{s}+q_{s-1})((m+1)q_{s}+q_{s-1})},\end{split}



and





 \begin{split}\lambda(I^{\prime}(b/k,m))&=\left|[0;b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{s-1},b_{s}-1,1,m]-[0;b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{s-1},b_{s}-1,1,m+1]\right|\\
&=\left|\frac{mp_{s}+p_{s}-p_{s-1}}{mq_{s}+q_{s}-q_{s-1}}-\frac{(m+1)p_{s}+p_{s}-p_{s-1}}{(m+1)q_{s}+q_{s}-q_{s-1}}\right|\\
&=\frac{1}{((m+1)q_{s}-q_{s-1})((m+2)q_{s}-q_{s-1})}.\end{split} λ⁡(I′​(b/k,m))=|[0;b1,b2,…,bs−1,bs−1,1,m]−[0;b1,b2,…,bs−1,bs−1,1,m+1]|=|m​ps+ps−ps−1m​qs+qs−qs−1−(m+1)​ps+ps−ps−1(m+1)​qs+qs−qs−1|=1((m+1)​qs−qs−1)​((m+2)​qs−qs−1).\begin{split}\lambda(I^{\prime}(b/k,m))&=\left|[0;b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{s-1},b_{s}-1,1,m]-[0;b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{s-1},b_{s}-1,1,m+1]\right|\\
&=\left|\frac{mp_{s}+p_{s}-p_{s-1}}{mq_{s}+q_{s}-q_{s-1}}-\frac{(m+1)p_{s}+p_{s}-p_{s-1}}{(m+1)q_{s}+q_{s}-q_{s-1}}\right|\\
&=\frac{1}{((m+1)q_{s}-q_{s-1})((m+2)q_{s}-q_{s-1})}.\end{split}



Since  p_{s}=b ps=bp_{s}=b and  q_{s}=k qs=kq_{s}=k, we have  bq_{s-1}\equiv(-1)^{s+1}\pmod{k} b​qs−1≡(−1)s+1(modk)bq_{s-1}\equiv(-1)^{s+1}\pmod{k}, thus the map  b\mapsto q_{s-1} b↦qs−1b\mapsto q_{s-1} is a bijection of  \mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*} ℤk∗\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}. Therefore





 \begin{split}&\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}}\int_{0}^{1}w_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},x\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&=\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}\frac{f(m)}{k^{2}}\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}}\left(\frac{1}{(m+\frac{b}{k})(m+1+\frac{b}{k})}+\frac{1}{(m+1-\frac{b}{k})(m+2-\frac{b}{k})}\right).\end{split} ∑b∈ℤk∗∫01wf,η,θ​(bk,x)​𝑑x=∑m=ηθf⁡(m)k2​∑b∈ℤk∗(1(m+bk)​(m+1+bk)+1(m+1−bk)​(m+2−bk)).\begin{split}&\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}}\int_{0}^{1}w_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},x\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&=\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}\frac{f(m)}{k^{2}}\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}}\left(\frac{1}{(m+\frac{b}{k})(m+1+\frac{b}{k})}+\frac{1}{(m+1-\frac{b}{k})(m+2-\frac{b}{k})}\right).\end{split}



Applying Lemma 8 with  T=(\log k)^{101} T=(log⁡k)101T=(\log k)^{101} and  u=2+\log k/(202\log\log k) u=2+log⁡k/(202​log⁡log⁡k)u=2+\log k/(202\log\log k) shows that the discrepancy of the set  \{b/k\,:\,b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}\} {b/k:b∈ℤk∗}\{b/k\,:\,b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}\} is  \ll 1/(\log k)^{100} ≪1/(log⁡k)100\ll 1/(\log k)^{100}. Lemma 9 thus gives





 \begin{split}\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}}&\left(\frac{1}{(m+\frac{b}{k})(m+1+\frac{b}{k})}+\frac{1}{(m+1-\frac{b}{k})(m+2-\frac{b}{k})}\right)\\
&=\varphi(k)\int_{0}^{1}\left(\frac{1}{(m+x)(m+1+x)}+\frac{1}{(m+1-x)(m+2-x)}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x+O\left(\frac{k}{(\log k)^{100}m^{2}}\right)\\
&=2\varphi(k)\log\left(1+\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\right)+O\left(\frac{k}{(\log k)^{100}m^{2}}\right),\end{split} ∑b∈ℤk∗(1(m+bk)​(m+1+bk)+1(m+1−bk)​(m+2−bk))=φ⁡(k)​∫01(1(m+x)​(m+1+x)+1(m+1−x)​(m+2−x))​dx+O⁡(k(log⁡k)100​m2)=2​φ​(k)​log⁡(1+1m⁡(m+2))+O⁡(k(log⁡k)100​m2),\begin{split}\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}}&\left(\frac{1}{(m+\frac{b}{k})(m+1+\frac{b}{k})}+\frac{1}{(m+1-\frac{b}{k})(m+2-\frac{b}{k})}\right)\\
&=\varphi(k)\int_{0}^{1}\left(\frac{1}{(m+x)(m+1+x)}+\frac{1}{(m+1-x)(m+2-x)}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x+O\left(\frac{k}{(\log k)^{100}m^{2}}\right)\\
&=2\varphi(k)\log\left(1+\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\right)+O\left(\frac{k}{(\log k)^{100}m^{2}}\right),\end{split}



and the claim follows.
∎

6. Proof of the main theorems



Proof of Theorems 3 and 5.



Let  f(x)=1 f⁡(x)=1f(x)=1. Note that with the notation of Theorem 12, we have  A_{f,\eta,\theta}=\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}\log\left(1+\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\right) Af,η,θ=12π2​∑m=ηθlog⁡(1+1m⁡(m+2))A_{f,\eta,\theta}=\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}\log\left(1+\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\right) and  B_{f,\eta,\theta}\ll 1/\eta Bf,η,θ≪1/ηB_{f,\eta,\theta}\ll 1/\eta. If  c\leq(\log N)^{C+1} c≤(log⁡N)C+1c\leq(\log N)^{C+1}, then by choosing  \eta=b η=b\eta=b and  \theta=c θ=c\theta=c we have  L_{[b,c]}(a/N)=S_{f,\eta,\theta}(a/N) L[b,c]​(a/N)=Sf,η,θ​(a/N)L_{[b,c]}(a/N)=S_{f,\eta,\theta}(a/N), and Theorem 12 immediately yields (3). If  c>(\log N)^{C+1} c>(log⁡N)C+1c>(\log N)^{C+1}, then by choosing  \eta=b η=b\eta=b and  \theta=\lfloor(\log N)^{C+1}\rfloor θ=⌊(log⁡N)C+1⌋\theta=\lfloor(\log N)^{C+1}\rfloor we have  S_{f,\eta,\theta}(a/N)\leq L_{[b,c]}(a/N)\leq S_{f,\eta,\theta}(a/N)+L_{[\theta+1,N]}(a/N) Sf,η,θ​(a/N)≤L[b,c]​(a/N)≤Sf,η,θ​(a/N)+L[θ+1,N]​(a/N)S_{f,\eta,\theta}(a/N)\leq L_{[b,c]}(a/N)\leq S_{f,\eta,\theta}(a/N)+L_{[\theta+1,N]}(a/N), and Theorem 12 yields





 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}L_{[b,c]}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)=\mu_{[b,\theta]}\log N+O\left(\frac{(\log\log N)^{3}}{b}+\frac{\log N\log\log N}{\theta}\right). 1φ⁡(N)​∑a∈ℤN∗L[b,c]​(aN)=μ[b,θ]​log⁡N+O⁡((log⁡log⁡N)3b+log⁡N​log⁡log​Nθ).\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}L_{[b,c]}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)=\mu_{[b,\theta]}\log N+O\left(\frac{(\log\log N)^{3}}{b}+\frac{\log N\log\log N}{\theta}\right).



Here  \mu_{[b,\theta]}=\mu_{[b,c]}+O(1/\theta) μ[b,θ]=μ[b,c]+O⁡(1/θ)\mu_{[b,\theta]}=\mu_{[b,c]}+O(1/\theta), and  \frac{\log N\log\log N}{\theta}\ll(\log\log N)^{3}/b log⁡N​log⁡log​Nθ≪(log⁡log⁡N)3/b\frac{\log N\log\log N}{\theta}\ll(\log\log N)^{3}/b. This finishes the proof of (3).




Observing that  M(a/N)\geq t\log N\,\Leftrightarrow\,L_{[\lceil t\log N\rceil,N]}(a/N)\geq 1 M⁡(a/N)≥t​log⁡N⇔L[⌈t​log⁡N⌉,N]​(a/N)≥1M(a/N)\geq t\log N\,\Leftrightarrow\,L_{[\lceil t\log N\rceil,N]}(a/N)\geq 1, an application of the Markov inequality leads to Theorem 3: for any  0<t\leq(\log N)^{C} 0<t≤(log⁡N)C0<t\leq(\log N)^{C}, we have





 \begin{split}\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\bigg|\bigg\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,&M\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\geq t\log N\bigg\}\bigg|\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}L_{[\lceil t\log N\rceil,N]}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\\
&=\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\log N\sum_{m\geq t\log N}\log\left(1+\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\right)+O\left(\frac{(\log\log N)^{3}}{t\log N}\right)\\
&=\frac{12}{\pi^{2}t}+O\left(\frac{(\log\log N)^{3}}{t\log N}\right).\end{split} 1φ⁡(N)|{a∈ℤN∗:M(aN)≥tlogN}|≤1φ⁡(N)​∑a∈ℤN∗L[⌈t​log⁡N⌉,N]​(aN)=12π2​log⁡N​∑m≥t​log⁡Nlog⁡(1+1m⁡(m+2))+O⁡((log⁡log⁡N)3t​log⁡N)=12π2​t+O⁡((log⁡log⁡N)3t​log⁡N).\begin{split}\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\bigg|\bigg\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,&M\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\geq t\log N\bigg\}\bigg|\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}L_{[\lceil t\log N\rceil,N]}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\\
&=\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\log N\sum_{m\geq t\log N}\log\left(1+\frac{1}{m(m+2)}\right)+O\left(\frac{(\log\log N)^{3}}{t\log N}\right)\\
&=\frac{12}{\pi^{2}t}+O\left(\frac{(\log\log N)^{3}}{t\log N}\right).\end{split}






It remains to prove the upper bound for the variance of  L_{[b,c]}(a/N) L[b,c]​(a/N)L_{[b,c]}(a/N). With the notation of Theorem 14 we have  D_{f,\eta,\theta}\ll 1/\eta^{4} Df,η,θ≪1/η4D_{f,\eta,\theta}\ll 1/\eta^{4}. If  c<(\log N)^{4C+1} c<(log⁡N)4​C+1c<(\log N)^{4C+1}, then choosing  \eta=b η=b\eta=b and  \theta=c θ=c\theta=c we have  L_{[b,c]}(a/N)=S_{f,\eta,\theta}(a/N) L[b,c]​(a/N)=Sf,η,θ​(a/N)L_{[b,c]}(a/N)=S_{f,\eta,\theta}(a/N), and Theorem 14 immediately yields





 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\left(L_{[b,c]}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-E\right)^{2}\ll\frac{(\log N)^{2}}{b^{4}}+\frac{\log N\log\log N}{b} 1φ⁡(N)​∑a∈ℤN∗(L[b,c]​(aN)−E)2≪(log⁡N)2b4+log⁡N​log⁡log​Nb\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\left(L_{[b,c]}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-E\right)^{2}\ll\frac{(\log N)^{2}}{b^{4}}+\frac{\log N\log\log N}{b}



with  E=\varphi(N)^{-1}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}L_{[b,c]}(a/N)=\mu_{[b,c]}\log N+O((\log\log N)^{3}/b) E=φ​(N)−1​∑a∈ℤN∗L[b,c]​(a/N)=μ[b,c]​log⁡N+O⁡((log⁡log⁡N)3/b)E=\varphi(N)^{-1}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}L_{[b,c]}(a/N)=\mu_{[b,c]}\log N+O((\log\log N)^{3}/b). The previous formula thus also holds with  E EE replaced by  \mu_{[b,c]}\log N μ[b,c]​log⁡N\mu_{[b,c]}\log N, as claimed.




If  c>(\log N)^{4C+1} c>(log⁡N)4​C+1c>(\log N)^{4C+1}, then we choose  \eta=b η=b\eta=b and  \theta=\lceil b^{4}\log N\rceil θ=⌈b4​log⁡N⌉\theta=\lceil b^{4}\log N\rceil. An application of Theorem 14 similarly leads to





 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\left(S_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-E\right)^{2}\ll\frac{(\log N)^{2}}{b^{4}}+\frac{\log N\log\log N}{b} 1φ⁡(N)​∑a∈ℤN∗(Sf,η,θ​(aN)−E)2≪(log⁡N)2b4+log⁡N​log⁡log​Nb\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\left(S_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-E\right)^{2}\ll\frac{(\log N)^{2}}{b^{4}}+\frac{\log N\log\log N}{b}



with  E=\varphi(N)^{-1}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}S_{f,\eta,\theta}(a/N)=\mu_{[b,c]}\log N+O((\log\log N)^{3}/b) E=φ​(N)−1​∑a∈ℤN∗Sf,η,θ​(a/N)=μ[b,c]​log⁡N+O⁡((log⁡log⁡N)3/b)E=\varphi(N)^{-1}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}S_{f,\eta,\theta}(a/N)=\mu_{[b,c]}\log N+O((\log\log N)^{3}/b). The previous formula thus also holds with  E EE replaced by  \mu_{[b,c]}\log N μ[b,c]​log⁡N\mu_{[b,c]}\log N. On the other hand, we have  L_{[b,c]}(a/N)\neq S_{f,\eta,\theta}(a/N)\,\Leftrightarrow\,M(a/N)>\theta L[b,c]​(a/N)≠Sf,η,θ​(a/N)⇔M⁡(a/N)>θL_{[b,c]}(a/N)\neq S_{f,\eta,\theta}(a/N)\,\Leftrightarrow\,M(a/N)>\theta, hence Theorem 3 gives





 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\left|\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,L_{[b,c]}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\neq S_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\right\}\right|\ll\frac{1}{b^{4}}. 1φ⁡(N)​|{a∈ℤN∗:L[b,c]​(aN)≠Sf,η,θ​(aN)}|≪1b4.\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\left|\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,L_{[b,c]}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\neq S_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\right\}\right|\ll\frac{1}{b^{4}}.



Using the uniform bound  L_{[b,c]}(a/N)\ll\log N L[b,c]​(a/N)≪log⁡NL_{[b,c]}(a/N)\ll\log N, the previous two formulas establish the desired upper estimate for the variance.
∎




Proof of Theorem 1.



The theorem trivially holds for  0<t<100 0<t<1000<t<100, hence we may assume that  100\leq t\leq(\log N)^{C} 100≤t≤(log⁡N)C100\leq t\leq(\log N)^{C}. Set  U=\lfloor\log_{2}(t\log N)\rfloor U=⌊log2⁡(t​log⁡N)⌋U=\lfloor\log_{2}(t\log N)\rfloor, and let  1\leq u\leq U 1≤u≤U1\leq u\leq U be an integer. Let  f(x)=x f⁡(x)=xf(x)=x,  \eta=2^{u-1} η=2u−1\eta=2^{u-1} and  \theta=2^{u}-1 θ=2u−1\theta=2^{u}-1, and consider  S^{(u)}(a/N):=S_{f,\eta,\theta}(a/N) S(u)​(a/N):=Sf,η,θ​(a/N)S^{(u)}(a/N):=S_{f,\eta,\theta}(a/N). Then





 A_{f,\eta,\theta}=\frac{12\log 2}{\pi^{2}}+O(2^{-u}),\quad B_{f,\eta,\theta}\ll 1,\quad C_{f,\eta,\theta}\ll 2^{u},\quad D_{f,\eta,\theta}\ll 2^{-2u}, Af,η,θ=12​log⁡2π2+O⁡(2−u),Bf,η,θ≪1,Cf,η,θ≪2u,Df,η,θ≪2−2​u,A_{f,\eta,\theta}=\frac{12\log 2}{\pi^{2}}+O(2^{-u}),\quad B_{f,\eta,\theta}\ll 1,\quad C_{f,\eta,\theta}\ll 2^{u},\quad D_{f,\eta,\theta}\ll 2^{-2u},



as well as  B_{f^{2},\eta,\theta}\ll 2^{u} Bf2,η,θ≪2uB_{f^{2},\eta,\theta}\ll 2^{u}, and  C_{f^{2},\eta,\theta}\ll 2^{u} Cf2,η,θ≪2uC_{f^{2},\eta,\theta}\ll 2^{u}. By Theorems 12 and 14, for all  1\leq u\leq U 1≤u≤U1\leq u\leq U, we have





 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}S^{(u)}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)=\frac{12\log 2}{\pi^{2}}\log N+O\left(2^{-u}\log N+(\log\log N)^{3}\right), 1φ⁡(N)​∑a∈ℤN∗S(u)​(aN)=12​log⁡2π2​log⁡N+O⁡(2−u​log⁡N+(log⁡log⁡N)3),\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}S^{(u)}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)=\frac{12\log 2}{\pi^{2}}\log N+O\left(2^{-u}\log N+(\log\log N)^{3}\right),



and




(39)

 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\left(S^{(u)}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-\frac{12\log 2}{\pi^{2}}\log N\right)^{2}\ll 2^{u}\log N+2^{-2u}(\log N)^{2}. 1φ⁡(N)​∑a∈ℤN∗(S(u)​(aN)−12​log⁡2π2​log⁡N)2≪2u​log⁡N+2−2​u​(log⁡N)2.\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\left(S^{(u)}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-\frac{12\log 2}{\pi^{2}}\log N\right)^{2}\ll 2^{u}\log N+2^{-2u}(\log N)^{2}.



An application of the Chebyshev inequality thus gives





 \begin{split}\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\bigg|\bigg\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,\bigg|S^{(u)}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-\frac{12\log 2}{\pi^{2}}&\log N\bigg|\geq\frac{t\log N}{10(\min\{u,U-u\})^{2}}\bigg\}\bigg|\\
&\ll\left(2^{u}\log N+2^{-2u}(\log N)^{2}\right)\cdot\frac{(\min\{u,U-u\})^{4}}{t^{2}(\log N)^{2}}\\
&\ll\frac{2^{u}(U-u)^{4}}{t^{2}\log N}+\frac{2^{-2u}u^{4}}{t^{2}}.\end{split} 1φ⁡(N)|{a∈ℤN∗:|S(u)(aN)−12​log⁡2π2logN|≥t​log⁡N10​(min⁡{u,U−u})2}|≪(2u​log⁡N+2−2​u​(log⁡N)2)⋅(min⁡{u,U−u})4t2​(log⁡N)2≪2u​(U−u)4t2​log⁡N+2−2​u​u4t2.\begin{split}\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\bigg|\bigg\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,\bigg|S^{(u)}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-\frac{12\log 2}{\pi^{2}}&\log N\bigg|\geq\frac{t\log N}{10(\min\{u,U-u\})^{2}}\bigg\}\bigg|\\
&\ll\left(2^{u}\log N+2^{-2u}(\log N)^{2}\right)\cdot\frac{(\min\{u,U-u\})^{4}}{t^{2}(\log N)^{2}}\\
&\ll\frac{2^{u}(U-u)^{4}}{t^{2}\log N}+\frac{2^{-2u}u^{4}}{t^{2}}.\end{split}



Since





 \sum_{u=1}^{U}\frac{1}{10(\min\{u,U-u\})^{2}}\leq\pi^{2}/30\leq 1/2, ∑u=1U110​(min⁡{u,U−u})2≤π2/30≤1/2,\sum_{u=1}^{U}\frac{1}{10(\min\{u,U-u\})^{2}}\leq\pi^{2}/30\leq 1/2,



the union bound immediately yields





 \begin{split}\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\bigg|\bigg\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,\bigg|\sum_{u=1}^{U}S^{(u)}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-\frac{12\log 2}{\pi^{2}}U&\log N\bigg|\geq\frac{t\log N}{2}\bigg\}\bigg|\\
&\ll\sum_{u=1}^{U}\left(\frac{2^{u}(U-u)^{4}}{t^{2}\log N}+\frac{u^{4}2^{-2u}}{t^{2}}\right)\ll\frac{1}{t}.\end{split} 1φ⁡(N)|{a∈ℤN∗:|∑u=1US(u)(aN)−12​log⁡2π2UlogN|≥t​log⁡N2}|≪∑u=1U(2u​(U−u)4t2​log⁡N+u4​2−2​ut2)≪1t.\begin{split}\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\bigg|\bigg\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,\bigg|\sum_{u=1}^{U}S^{(u)}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-\frac{12\log 2}{\pi^{2}}U&\log N\bigg|\geq\frac{t\log N}{2}\bigg\}\bigg|\\
&\ll\sum_{u=1}^{U}\left(\frac{2^{u}(U-u)^{4}}{t^{2}\log N}+\frac{u^{4}2^{-2u}}{t^{2}}\right)\ll\frac{1}{t}.\end{split}






On the other hand,





 S\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)=\sum_{u=1}^{U}S^{(u)}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathds{1}_{\{2^{U}\leq a_{i}\}}a_{i}, S(aN)=∑u=1US(u)(aN)+∑i=1r𝟙{2U≤ai}ai,S\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)=\sum_{u=1}^{U}S^{(u)}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathds{1}_{\{2^{U}\leq a_{i}\}}a_{i},



and in particular,  S(a/N)\neq\sum_{u=1}^{U}S^{(u)}(a/N)\,\Leftrightarrow\,M(a/N)\geq 2^{U} S⁡(a/N)≠∑u=1US(u)​(a/N)⇔M⁡(a/N)≥2US(a/N)\neq\sum_{u=1}^{U}S^{(u)}(a/N)\,\Leftrightarrow\,M(a/N)\geq 2^{U}. Theorem 3 thus shows that





 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\left|\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,S\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\neq\sum_{u=1}^{U}S^{(u)}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\right\}\right|\ll\frac{1}{t}, 1φ⁡(N)​|{a∈ℤN∗:S⁡(aN)≠∑u=1US(u)​(aN)}|≪1t,\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\left|\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,S\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\neq\sum_{u=1}^{U}S^{(u)}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)\right\}\right|\ll\frac{1}{t},



and consequently,





 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\left|\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,\left|S\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-\frac{12\log 2}{\pi^{2}}U\log N\right|\geq\frac{t\log N}{2}\right\}\right|\ll\frac{1}{t}. 1φ⁡(N)​|{a∈ℤN∗:|S⁡(aN)−12​log⁡2π2​U​log⁡N|≥t​log⁡N2}|≪1t.\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\left|\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,\left|S\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-\frac{12\log 2}{\pi^{2}}U\log N\right|\geq\frac{t\log N}{2}\right\}\right|\ll\frac{1}{t}.



Finally, note that





 \frac{12}{\pi^{2}}|\log\log N-U\log 2|\leq\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}(1+\log t)\leq t/2 12π2​|log⁡log⁡N−U​log⁡2|≤12π2​(1+log⁡t)≤t/2\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}|\log\log N-U\log 2|\leq\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}(1+\log t)\leq t/2



holds by the assumption  t\geq 100 t≥100t\geq 100. Overall, we conclude that





 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\left|\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,\left|S\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\log N\log\log N\right|\geq t\log N\right\}\right|\ll\frac{1}{t}, 1φ⁡(N)​|{a∈ℤN∗:|S⁡(aN)−12π2​log​N​log⁡log⁡N|≥t​log​N}|≪1t,\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\left|\left\{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}\,:\,\left|S\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)-\frac{12}{\pi^{2}}\log N\log\log N\right|\geq t\log N\right\}\right|\ll\frac{1}{t},



as desired.
∎

Note that the parity of  i ii is equivalent to whether  \frac{p_{i}}{q_{i}} piqi\frac{p_{i}}{q_{i}} approximates  \frac{a}{N} aN\frac{a}{N}
from the left or from the right. Hence, we can count partial quotients with even index by

smaller than  b/k b/kb/k (in contrast to the two-sided weight functions in Section 3). This will allow us to obtain a non-trivial estimate
on the variance of  S^{\prime}_{\textup{e}} Se′S^{\prime}_{\textup{e}}, and lead to the desired bound on the second moment of  S_{\mathrm{alt}} SaltS_{\mathrm{alt}}.

2\leq q_{2i-1}<\frac{\sqrt{N}}{e^{c(T)}}\end{subarray}}^{\lfloor r/2\rfloor}\mathds{1}_{\{\eta\leq a_{2i}\leq\theta\}}a_{2i}\cdot S_{\textup{e}}^{(\geq 2)}\left(\frac{p_{2i-1}}{q_{2i-1}}\right)\\
&\quad+\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathds{1}_{\{\eta\leq a_{i}\leq\theta\}}a_{i}^{2}+2\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
\frac{\sqrt{N}}{e^{c(T)}}\leq q_{i-1}<\sqrt{N}\end{subarray}}^{r}\mathds{1}_{\{\eta\leq a_{i}\leq\theta\}}a_{i}\left(S_{f,\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{p_{i-1}}{q_{i-1}}\right)+\mathds{1}_{\{a_{i-2}=\theta\}}\theta\right)\\
&\quad+2\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
2\leq q_{i-1}<\frac{\sqrt{N}}{e^{c(T)}}\end{subarray}}^{\lfloor r/2\rfloor}\mathds{1}_{\{\eta\leq a_{i}\leq\theta\}}a_{i}\mathds{1}_{\{a_{i-2}=\theta\}}\theta,\end{split}



where  T TT and  c(T) c⁡(T)c(T) are chosen as in the proof of Theorem 14.
Everything except the first term in this equation can be estimated as in the proof of Lemma 11 resp. Theorem 14.
Counting partial quotients with even indices is equivalent to only considering convergents  b/k b/kb/k for which  b/k-a/N>0 b/k−a/N>0b/k-a/N>0, because  p_{2i}/q_{2i}<a/N<p_{2i-1}/q_{2i-1} p2​i/q2​i<a/N<p2​i−1/q2​i−1p_{2i}/q_{2i}<a/N<p_{2i-1}/q_{2i-1}.
Accordingly we define  I_{\textup{left}}(b/k,m) Ileft​(b/k,m)I_{\textup{left}}(b/k,m) to be the one interval out of  I(b/k,m) I⁡(b/k,m)I(b/k,m),  I^{\prime}(b/k,m) I′​(b/k,m)I^{\prime}(b/k,m), that lies to the left of  b/k b/kb/k. That is,








 I_{\textup{left}}(b/k,m):=\begin{cases}I(b/k,m)&\text{ if }I(b/k,m)\subseteq[0,b/k],\\
I^{\prime}(b/k,m)&\text{ if }I(b/k,m)\subseteq[b/k,1].\end{cases} Ileft​(b/k,m):={I⁡(b/k,m) if ​I​(b/k,m)⊆[0,b/k],I′​(b/k,m) if ​I​(b/k,m)⊆[b/k,1].I_{\textup{left}}(b/k,m):=\begin{cases}I(b/k,m)&\text{ if }I(b/k,m)\subseteq[0,b/k],\\
I^{\prime}(b/k,m)&\text{ if }I(b/k,m)\subseteq[b/k,1].\end{cases}



Further, we define the one-sided weight function








 w_{\textup{e},\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},x\right)=\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}m\cdot\mathds{1}_{I_{\textup{left}}(b/k,m)}\left(x\right) we,η,θ​(bk,x)=∑m=ηθm⋅𝟙Ileft​(b/k,m)​(x)w_{\textup{e},\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},x\right)=\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}m\cdot\mathds{1}_{I_{\textup{left}}(b/k,m)}\left(x\right)



to obtain








 \sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
2\leq q_{2i-1}<\sqrt{N}\end{subarray}}^{\lfloor r/2\rfloor}\mathds{1}_{\{\eta\leq a_{2i}\leq\theta\}}a_{2i}\cdot S_{\textup{e}}^{(\geq 2)}\left(\frac{p_{2i-1}}{q_{2i-1}}\right)=\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\sum_{2\leq k<\sqrt{N}}\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}}w_{\textup{e},\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},\frac{a}{N}\right)S_{\textup{e}}^{(\geq 2)}\left(\frac{b}{k}\right). ∑a∈ℤN∗∑i=12≤q2​i−1<N⌊r/2⌋𝟙{η≤a2​i≤θ}a2​i⋅Se(≥2)(p2​i−1q2​i−1)=∑a∈ℤN∗∑2≤k<N∑b∈ℤk∗we,η,θ(bk,aN)Se(≥2)(bk).\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
2\leq q_{2i-1}<\sqrt{N}\end{subarray}}^{\lfloor r/2\rfloor}\mathds{1}_{\{\eta\leq a_{2i}\leq\theta\}}a_{2i}\cdot S_{\textup{e}}^{(\geq 2)}\left(\frac{p_{2i-1}}{q_{2i-1}}\right)=\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\sum_{2\leq k<\sqrt{N}}\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}}w_{\textup{e},\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},\frac{a}{N}\right)S_{\textup{e}}^{(\geq 2)}\left(\frac{b}{k}\right).



Using an analogue of (36) and





 \int_{0}^{1}w_{\textup{e},\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},x\right)\,\mathrm{d}x=\frac{\pi^{2}A_{f,\eta,\theta}}{12k^{2}}+O\left(\frac{1}{k^{2}}\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}\frac{f(m)}{m^{3}}\right), ∫01we,η,θ​(bk,x)​𝑑x=π2​Af,η,θ12​k2+O⁡(1k2​∑m=ηθf⁡(m)m3),\int_{0}^{1}w_{\textup{e},\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},x\right)\,\mathrm{d}x=\frac{\pi^{2}A_{f,\eta,\theta}}{12k^{2}}+O\left(\frac{1}{k^{2}}\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}\frac{f(m)}{m^{3}}\right),



we get





 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}w_{\textup{e},\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},\frac{a}{N}\right)=\frac{\pi^{2}A_{f,\eta,\theta}}{12k^{2}}+O\left(\frac{1}{k^{2}}\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}\frac{f(m)}{m^{3}}+\frac{f(\theta)\log N}{k^{2}T}+\frac{f(\theta)e^{c(T)}}{\varphi(N)}\right). 1φ⁡(N)​∑a∈ℤN∗we,η,θ​(bk,aN)=π2​Af,η,θ12​k2+O⁡(1k2​∑m=ηθf⁡(m)m3+f⁡(θ)​log⁡Nk2​T+f⁡(θ)​ec⁡(T)φ⁡(N)).\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}w_{\textup{e},\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},\frac{a}{N}\right)=\frac{\pi^{2}A_{f,\eta,\theta}}{12k^{2}}+O\left(\frac{1}{k^{2}}\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}\frac{f(m)}{m^{3}}+\frac{f(\theta)\log N}{k^{2}T}+\frac{f(\theta)e^{c(T)}}{\varphi(N)}\right).



Summing over  b bb and  k kk and applying Theorem 12 for fixed denominator  k kk, together with (63) and (64) this shows
analogously to (38)
that





 \begin{split}&\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\sum_{2\leq k<\frac{\sqrt{N}}{e^{c(T)}}}\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}}w_{\textup{e},\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},\frac{a}{N}\right)S_{\textup{e}}^{(\geq 2)}\left(\frac{b}{k}\right)\\
&\quad\leq\frac{A_{f,\eta,\theta}^{2}}{32}(\log N)^{2}+O\left(D^{\prime}_{f,\eta,\theta}(\log N)^{2}+B_{f,\eta,\theta}^{2}\log N(\log\log N)^{3}\right),\end{split} ∑a∈ℤN∗∑2≤k<Nec⁡(T)∑b∈ℤk∗we,η,θ​(bk,aN)​Se(≥2)​(bk)≤Af,η,θ232​(log⁡N)2+O⁡(Df,η,θ′​(log⁡N)2+Bf,η,θ2​log⁡N​(log⁡log⁡N)3),\begin{split}&\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}\sum_{2\leq k<\frac{\sqrt{N}}{e^{c(T)}}}\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{*}}w_{\textup{e},\eta,\theta}\left(\frac{b}{k},\frac{a}{N}\right)S_{\textup{e}}^{(\geq 2)}\left(\frac{b}{k}\right)\\
&\quad\leq\frac{A_{f,\eta,\theta}^{2}}{32}(\log N)^{2}+O\left(D^{\prime}_{f,\eta,\theta}(\log N)^{2}+B_{f,\eta,\theta}^{2}\log N(\log\log N)^{3}\right),\end{split}



where  D^{\prime}_{f,\eta,\theta}:=B_{f,\eta,\theta}\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}\frac{f(m)}{m^{3}}. Df,η,θ′:=Bf,η,θ​∑m=ηθf⁡(m)m3.D^{\prime}_{f,\eta,\theta}:=B_{f,\eta,\theta}\sum_{m=\eta}^{\theta}\frac{f(m)}{m^{3}}.
Combining the previous estimates proves the desired result.
∎




Proof of Theorem 6.



We can now prove Theorem 6 in the precisely same fashion as
Theorem 1, with the only difference that
(39) is replaced by




(67)

 \frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}S_{\mathrm{alt},2^{u-1},2^{u}-1}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)^{2}\ll 2^{u}\log N+\frac{(\log N)^{2}}{2^{u}}, 1φ⁡(N)​∑a∈ℤN∗Salt,2u−1,2u−1​(aN)2≪2u​log⁡N+(log⁡N)22u,\frac{1}{\varphi(N)}\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}^{*}}S_{\mathrm{alt},2^{u-1},2^{u}-1}\left(\frac{a}{N}\right)^{2}\ll 2^{u}\log N+\frac{(\log N)^{2}}{2^{u}},



which we obtain by an application of Lemma 15 with  \eta=2^{u-1},\theta=2^{u}-1 η=2u−1,θ=2u−1\eta=2^{u-1},\theta=2^{u}-1.
∎
